{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m^{\prime}
, where either
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
or
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

Case:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
and
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

We discharge this case by showing a contradiction to the assumption that
m
is at the head of the channel between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.

Because
\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{pre}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and
\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}<\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
from the claim above, it must be the case that

\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{pre}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{post}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m^{\prime}
is in
w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}}
.
From Lemma 19
[
28
]
, it follows that

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}?\_}).m^{\prime}.u^{\prime}
and

\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})=m^{\prime}.u^{\prime}
, i.e.
m^{\prime}
is at the head of the channel between
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
This contradicts the assumption that
m
is at the head of
\xi({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})
.

Case:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
.

We discharge this case by showing a contradiction to Receive Validity.
We instantiate Receive Validity with

\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{x}\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}

to obtain

\forall~\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2}\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\;\implies\;\forall~G_{2}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2}).\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m\notin M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G_{2}\ldots)}\kern 5.0pt.

We prove the negation, stated as follows:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\land\exists~s_{2}\in Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},G_{2}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}?m^{\prime}}s_{2}).\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G_{2}\ldots)}\kern 5.0pt.

The left conjunct follows immediately.
From the existence of
\rho^{\prime}
and Lemma
1
, there exists an
s_{2}
such that

\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}?m^{\prime}}s_{2}\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
The fact that

G^{\prime}_{post}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}?m^{\prime}}s_{2})
is trivial from the unique splitting of
\rho^{\prime}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
matching
w
:

\rho^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\cdot\beta^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore, all that remains is to show that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\ldots)}
.
Because

\rho^{\prime}\in\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}(w)

and

\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{post}\leq\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is not the active role in
l^{\prime}
,
there must exist a transition labeled
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m

that occurs in the suffix
G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\cdot\beta^{\prime}
of
\rho^{\prime}
.
Let

G_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m}G_{0}^{\prime}

be the earliest occurrence of such a transition in the suffix, then:

\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\ldots G_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m}G_{0}^{\prime}\dots\kern 5.0pt.

Note that
G_{0}
must be a syntactic subterm of
G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}
.
In order for

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\ldots)}
.
to hold, it suffices to show that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}

in the recursive call to

M^{\mathcal{B}}_{(G_{0}\dots)}
.

We argue this from the definition of
M
and the fact that
\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{pre}
.
Suppose for the sake of contradiction that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\in\mathcal{B}
.
Because
M
only adds receivers of already blocked senders to
\mathcal{B}
and
M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\ldots)}
starts with
\mathcal{B}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}
, there must exist a chain of message exchanges
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}:m_{i}
in
G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}
with
1\leq i<n
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{n}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{1}
. That is,
G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\cdot\beta^{\prime}
must be of the form

G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\dots G_{n-1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{n-1}:m_{n-1}}G_{n-1}^{\prime}\ldots G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m_{1}}G_{1}^{\prime}\ldots G_{0}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m}G_{0}^{\prime}\ldots\kern 5.0pt.

Let
m_{0}=m
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
. We show by induction over
i
that for all
i\in[1,n]

\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\dots G_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i-1}:m_{i-1}}G_{i}^{\prime}~\leq~\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}}\kern 5.0pt.

We then obtain the desired contradiction with the fact that
\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{n}}=\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}
.

The base case of the induction follows immediately from the construction. For the induction step, assume that

\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\dots G_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i-1}:m_{i-1}}G_{i}^{\prime}~\leq~\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}}\kern 5.0pt.

From the definition of
\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}}
and the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}
is the active role in
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}?m_{i}
, it follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}?m_{i}\in w
. Hence, we must also have
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}!m_{i}\in w
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}
is the active role in
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}!m_{i}
, we can conclude

\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime}_{\mathit{post}}\dots G_{i}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i}:m_{i}}G_{i+1}^{\prime}~\leq~\rho^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}_{i+1}}\kern 5.0pt.

∎

See
5

Proof

Let
x
=
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
.
We prove the claim by induction on the length of
w
.

Base Case.

w=\varepsilon
.
By definition,
I(\varepsilon)
contains all maximal runs in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
, and the unique splitting prefix of any run
\rho\in I(\varepsilon)
for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
with respect to
\varepsilon
is
\varepsilon
.
Because
\varepsilon
is a prefix of any run, we need only show the non-emptiness of
I(x)
.
By
1
,
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
.
Because
x
is the prefix of a word in
\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
, there exists
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
such that

x\leq w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
.
By the semantics of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
, there exists a run
\rho^{\prime}\in\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
such that

x
is the first symbol in
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
, and therefore

\rho^{\prime}\in I(x)
.

Induction Step.

Let
wx
be an extension of
w
by
x\in\Sigma_{!}
.

Let
\rho
be a run in
I(w)
, and let
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{l}G^{\prime}\cdot\beta
be the unique splitting of
\rho
for role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
with respect to
w
.
To re-establish the induction hypothesis, we need to show the existence of a run
\bar{\rho}
in
I(wx)
such that

\alpha\cdot G\leq\bar{\rho}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is the active role in
x
, it holds for any
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
that

\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}(w)=\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}(wx)
.
Therefore, to prove the existential claim, it suffices to construct a run
\bar{\rho}
that satisfies:

1.

\bar{\rho}\in\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}(wx)
,

2.

\bar{\rho}\in I(w)
, and

3.

\alpha\cdot G\leq\bar{\rho}
.

In the case that
l{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}=x
, we
are done:
Property
3
and
2
hold by construction, and Property
1
holds by the definition of possible run sets.
In the case that
l{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\neq x
, we show the existence of a transition label and state
\xrightarrow{\bar{l}}\bar{G^{\prime}}
, and a maximal suffix
\bar{\beta}
such that
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{\bar{l}}\bar{G^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}
satisfies all three conditions.

Let (
\vec{s}_{w},\xi_{w})
denote the CSM configuration reached on
w
:

(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{w}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s}_{w},\xi_{w})

Send Validity states that every transition in
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
originates in all global states in
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
By assumption,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
is a transition in
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
By Proposition
2
,
\rho\in I\subseteq\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}(w)
, and therefore
G\in\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Therefore, Send Validity gives the existence of some
\bar{G}^{\prime}\in Q_{\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})}
such that
G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G}^{\prime}\in\delta_{\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})}
.
Because
\alpha\cdot G
is a run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
and
G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G}^{\prime}
is a transition in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
,
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G}^{\prime}
is a run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.

The construction thus far satisfies Property
1
and
3
regardless of our choice of maximal suffix: for all choices of
\bar{\beta}
such that
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}
is a maximal run,
wx{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G}^{\prime}\cdot\bar{\beta})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
\alpha\cdot G\leq\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G}^{\prime}\cdot\bar{\beta}
.

Property
2
, however, requires that the projection of
w
onto each role is consistent with
\bar{\rho}
, and this cannot be ensured by the prefix alone.

We construct the remainder of
\bar{\rho}
by picking an arbitrary maximal suffix to form a candidate run, and iteratively performing suffix replacements on the candidate run until it lands in
I
.
Let
\bar{\beta}
be a run suffix such that
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}
is a maximal run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.
Let
\rho_{c}
denote our candidate run
\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}
.
If
\rho_{c}\in I
, we are done.
Otherwise,
\rho_{c}\notin I
and there exists a non-empty set of processes
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
such that for each
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{S}
,

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\nleq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(1)

By the fact that
\rho\in I
,

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho)){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(2)

We can rewrite (
1
) and (
2
) above as:

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}

\displaystyle\nleq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}:m}\bar{G^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

(3)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G\xrightarrow{l}G^{\prime}\cdot\beta)){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(4)

By the definition of unique splitting,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is the active role in
l
.
By Lemma
1
,

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
, and because

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho))\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
,
it holds that

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho)){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
and

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho)){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
.
By assumption,
\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
is in state
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
upon consuming
w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
.
Then, there must exist an outgoing transition from
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
labeled with
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(l){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
.
By No Mixed Choice (
Corollary

1
), all outgoing transitions from
\vec{s}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
must be send actions.
Therefore,
l
must be of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}\!:\!m^{\prime}
.
By assumption,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\lor m^{\prime}\neq m
.

We can further rewrite (
3
) and (
4
) to make explicit their shared prefix:

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\nleq(\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G)).~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m.~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m.~\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\bar{\beta}))){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

(5)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq(\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G)).~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}!m^{\prime}.~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m^{\prime}.~\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\beta))){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

(6)

It is clear that in order for both (
5
) and (
6
) to hold, it must be the case that

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G)){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
.

We formalize the point of disagreement between
w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
and
\rho_{c}
using an index
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
representing the position of the first disagreeing transition label in
\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c})
:

i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\coloneq\text{max}\{i\mid\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}[0..i-1])){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\}\kern 5.0pt.

Then,
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}[i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}])){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
and from (
5
) and (
6
) we know that
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}>2*|\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G))|
.

We identify the role in
\mathcal{S}
with the
earliest disagreement
in
\rho_{c}
: let
\bar{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
be the role with the smallest
i_{\bar{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
in
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
denote
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}[i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}])){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
.

Claim.

y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
must be a send event.

Assume by contradiction that
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
is a receive event.
We identify the symbol in
w
that disagrees with
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
: let
w^{\prime}
be the largest prefix of
w
such that
w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}))
.
By definition,
w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}=\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}-1])){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
.
Let
z
be the next symbol following
w^{\prime}
in
w
; then
z\in\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
and
z\neq y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
Furthermore, by No Mixed Choice (
1
) we have that
z\in\Sigma_{?}
.

By assumption,

w^{\prime}z\nleq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]))
.
Therefore, any run that begins with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
cannot be contained in
\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}(w^{\prime}z)
, or consequently in
I(w^{\prime}z)
.
We show however, that
I(w^{\prime}z)
must contain some runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
.
From Lemma
4
for traces
w^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}z
, we obtain that

I(w^{\prime})=I(w^{\prime}z)
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
I(w^{\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
.

Claim

\forall w^{\prime\prime}\leq w^{\prime}.\,I(w^{\prime\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
.

We prove the claim via induction on
w^{\prime}
.

The base case is trivial from the fact that
I(\varepsilon)
contains all maximal runs.

For the inductive step, let
w^{\prime\prime}y\leq w^{\prime}
.

In the case that
y\in\Sigma_{?}
, from Lemma
4

I(w^{\prime\prime}y)=I(w^{\prime\prime})
and the witness from
I(w^{\prime\prime})
can be reused.

In the case that
y\in\Sigma_{!}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
be the active role of
y
and let
\rho^{\prime}
be a run in
I(w^{\prime\prime})
beginning with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
given by the inner induction hypothesis.
Let
\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}G^{\prime\prime}\cdot\beta^{\prime}
be the unique splitting of
\rho^{\prime}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
with respect to
w^{\prime\prime}
.
If
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}}=y
, then
\rho^{\prime}
can be used as the witness.
Otherwise,
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}}\neq y
, and
\rho^{\prime}\notin\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}}(w^{\prime\prime}y)
.

The outer induction hypothesis holds for all prefixes of
w
: we instantiate it with
w^{\prime\prime}
and
y
to obtain:

\exists~\rho^{\prime\prime}\in I(w^{\prime\prime}y).~\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
be defined as before; it follows that
\rho^{\prime}[i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}]=G^{\prime}
.
It must be the case that
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}>i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
: if
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\leq i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
, because
\rho_{c}
and
\rho^{\prime}
share a prefix
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
and
w^{\prime\prime}y\leq w
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
would be the earliest disagreeing role instead of
\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

Because
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}>i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
,

\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]=\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]\leq\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}]
.
Because
\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}]=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot G^{\prime}\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}
,
it follows from prefix transitivity that
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}
,
thus re-establishing the induction hypothesis for
w^{\prime\prime}y
with
\rho^{\prime\prime}
as a witness run that begins with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
.

This concludes our proof that
I(w^{\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}]
, and in turn our proof by contradiction that
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
must be a receive event.

We can rewrite candidate run
\rho_{c}
as follows:

\rho_{c}=G_{0}\xrightarrow{l_{0}}G_{1}\ldots G_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\xrightarrow{l_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}G_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}+1}\ldots\kern 5.0pt.

We have established that
l_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
must be a send event for
\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
We can reason from Send Validity similarly to our construction of
\bar{\rho}
’s prefix above, and conclude that there exists a transition label and maximal suffix from
G_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
such that the resulting run no longer disagrees with
w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}}
.
We update our candidate run
\rho_{c}
with the correct transition label and maximal suffix, update the set of states
\mathcal{S}\in\mathcal{P}
to the new set of roles that disagree with the new candidate run, and repeat the construction above on the new candidate run until
\mathcal{S}
is empty.

Termination is guaranteed in at most
|w|
steps by the fact that the number of symbols in
w
that agree with the candidate run up to
i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
must increase.

Upon termination, the resulting
\bar{\rho}
satisfies the final remaining property
3
:
\bar{\rho}\in I
.
This concludes the proof of the inductive step, and consequently the proof of the prefix-preservation of send transitions.

∎

See
3

Proof

We prove the claim by induction on the length of
w
.

Base Case.

w=\varepsilon
.
The trace
w=\varepsilon
is trivially consistent with all maximal runs, and
I(w)
therefore contains all maximal runs.
By definition of
\mathbf{G}
, language
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
is non-empty and at least one maximal run exists.
Thus,
I(w)
is non-empty.

Induction Step.

Let
wx
be an extension of
w
by
x\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}
.

The induction hypothesis states that

I(w)\neq\emptyset
.
To re-establish the induction hypothesis, we need to show

I(wx)\neq\emptyset
.
We proceed by case analysis on whether
x
is a receive or send event.

Receive Case.

Let
x
=
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}?m
.
By
Lemma

4
,
I(wx)=I(w)
.
I(wx)\neq\emptyset
follows trivially from the induction hypothesis and this equality.

Send Case.

Let
x
=
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
.
By
Lemma

5
, there exists a run in
I(wx)
that shares a prefix with a run in
I(w)
.
I(wx)\neq\emptyset
again follows trivially.

∎

Lemma 7

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a global type and
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be the subset construction.
Let
w
be a trace of
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
. If
w
is terminated, then
w
is
\mathbf{G}
-complete.

Proof

We prove the claim by contraposition and assume that
w
is not
\mathbf{G}
-complete.
Then, there exists a run
\rho\in I(w)
and a non-empty set of roles
\mathcal{S}
such that for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{S}
, it holds that

w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\neq\bigl(\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho))\bigr){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
(*).
Since
w
is a trace, we know there exists a run

(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{w_{0}}\ldots\xrightarrow{w_{n-1}}(\vec{s}_{n},\xi_{n})

of
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

such that

w=w_{0}\ldots w_{n-1}
.
We need to show that there exists
(\vec{s}_{n+1},\xi_{n+1})
with

(\vec{s}_{n},\xi_{n})\xrightarrow{w_{n}}(\vec{s}_{n+1},\xi_{n+1})
for some
w_{n}
.
Given some role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, let
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
denote the largest prefix of
\rho
that contains
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
’s local view of
w
. Formally,

\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=max\{\rho^{\prime}~|~\rho^{\prime}\leq\rho~\land~\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\}\kern 5.0pt.

Note that due to maximality, the next transition in
\rho
after
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
must have
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
as its active role.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
be the role in
\mathcal{S}
for whom
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is the smallest.
From
Lemma

1
and (*), it follows that
\vec{s}_{n,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}
has outgoing transitions.
If
q_{n,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}
has outgoing send transitions, then
(q_{n+1},\xi_{n+1})
exists trivially.
If
q_{n,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}
has outgoing receive transitions, it must be the case that the next transition in
\rho
after
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}\!:\!m
for some
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and
m
.
From the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
is the role with the smallest
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
, we know that
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}<\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, and from the FIFO property of CSM channels it follows that
m
is in
\xi_{n}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}})
. Then, the receive transition is enabled for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}
, and there exists
(\vec{s}_{n+1},\xi_{n+1})
with

(\vec{s}_{n},\xi_{n})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m}(\vec{s}_{n+1},\xi_{n+1})
.
This shows that
w=w_{1}\ldots w_{n-1}
is not terminated and concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma 8

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a global type and
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be the subset projection.
Let
w
be a trace of
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
If
w
is
\mathbf{G}
-complete, then
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
.

Proof

By definition of
w
being
\mathbf{G}
-complete,

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P},~\rho\in I(w).~w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\bigl(\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho))\bigr){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

From
Lemma

3
,

I(w)
is non-empty.
Let
\rho
be a run in
I(w)
, and let

w^{\prime}=\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho))\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
.
By the semantics of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
,
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
is closed under the
\sim
relation, and thus it suffices to show that
w\sim w^{\prime}
.

[
28
, Lemma 23]
states that if
w
is channel-compliant
[
28
, Definition 19]
, then

w\sim w^{\prime}
iff
w^{\prime}
is channel-compliant and forall
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,

w{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
The fact that
w
is channel-compliant follows from
[
28
, Lemma 20]
and
w
being a a CSM trace;

w^{\prime}
is channel-compliant by construction, and the last condition is satisfied by assumption that
w
is
\mathbf{G}
-complete and by definition of
w^{\prime}
.
Thus, we conclude that
w\sim w^{\prime}
.

∎

See
6.1

Proof

First, we show that
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is deadlock-free, namely, that every finite trace extends to a maximal trace.
Let
w
be a trace of
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
Let
w^{\prime}
denote the extension of
w
.
If
w^{\prime}\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{\omega}
, then
w^{\prime}
is maximal and we are done.
Otherwise, we have
w^{\prime}\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
. Let
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
denote the
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration reached on
w^{\prime}
.
By definition of
w^{\prime}
being the largest extension,
w^{\prime}
is a terminated trace, and there exists no configuration reachable from
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.
By
Lemma

7
,
w^{\prime}
is
\mathbf{G}
-complete.
By
Lemma

8
,
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
.
Therefore, all states in
\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime}
are final and all channels in
\xi
are empty, and
w^{\prime}
is a maximal trace in
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

This concludes our proof that
\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is deadlock-free.

Next, we show that

\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})=\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
.
The backward direction,
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})\subseteq\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
, is given by
Lemma

2
.
For the forward direction, let
w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
, and let
(\vec{s},\xi)
denote the configuration reached on
w
.
We proceed by case analysis on whether
w
is a finite or infinite maximal trace.

Case:

w\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
.
We show a stronger property:
w
is a terminated trace.
Then, we use
Lemma

7
and
Lemma

8
as above to obtain
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
.
By definition of
(\vec{s},\xi)
being final, all states in
\vec{s}
are final and all channels in
\xi
are empty.
We argue there does not exist a configuration reachable from
(\vec{s},\xi)
.
From
Proposition

3
, all outgoing states from states in
\vec{s}
must be receive transitions.
However, no receive transitions are enabled because all channels in
\xi
are empty.
Therefore,
(\vec{s},\xi)
is a terminated configuration and
w
is a terminated trace.

Case:

w\in\Sigma^{\omega}
.
By the semantics of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
, to show
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
it suffices to show:

\exists w^{\prime}\in\Sigma^{\omega}.~w^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})))\land w\preceq_{\sim}^{\omega}w^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

Claim.

\bigcap_{u\leq w}I(u)
contains an infinite run.

First, we show that there exists an infinite run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.
We apply König’s Lemma to an infinite tree where each vertex corresponds to a finite run.
We obtain the vertex set from the intersection sets of
w
’s prefixes; each prefix “contributes” a set of finite runs.
Formally, for each prefix
u\leq w
, let
V_{u}
be defined as:

V_{u}\coloneq\bigcup_{\rho_{u}\in I(u)}\text{min}\{\rho^{\prime}\mid\rho^{\prime}\leq\rho_{u}\land\forall{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}.~u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\}\kern 5.0pt.

By
Lemma

3
,
V_{u}
is guaranteed to be non-empty.
We construct a tree
\mathcal{T}_{w}(V,E)
with

V\coloneq\bigcup_{u\leq w}V_{u}
and

E\coloneq\{(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\mid\rho_{1}\leq\rho_{2}\}
.
The tree is rooted in the empty run, which is included
V
by
V_{\varepsilon}
.

V
is infinite because there are infinitely many prefixes of
w
.

\mathcal{T}_{w}
is finitely branching due to the finiteness of
\delta_{\mathbf{G}}
and the fact that each vertex represents a finite run.
Therefore, there must exist a ray in
\mathcal{T}_{w}
representing an infinite run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.

Let
\rho^{\prime}
be such an infinite run.
We now show that
\rho^{\prime}\in\bigcap_{u\leq w}I(u)
.
Let
v
be a prefix of
w
.
To show that
\rho^{\prime}\in I(v)
, it suffices to show that one of the vertices in
V_{v}
lies on
\rho^{\prime}
. In other words,

V_{v}\cap\{v\mid v\in\rho^{\prime}\}\neq\emptyset\kern 5.0pt.

Assume by contradiction that
\rho^{\prime}
passes through none of the vertices in
V_{v}
.
Then, for any
u^{\prime}\geq u
, because intersection sets are monotonically decreasing, it must be the case that
\rho^{\prime}
passes through none of the vertices in
V_{u}^{\prime}
.
Therefore,
\rho^{\prime}
can only pass through vertices in
V_{u}^{\prime\prime}
, where
u^{\prime\prime}\leq u
.
However, the set
\bigcup_{u^{\prime\prime}\leq u}V_{u}^{\prime\prime}
has finite cardinality.
We reach a contradiction, concluding our proof of the above claim.

Let
\rho^{\prime}\in\bigcap_{u\leq w}I(u)
, and let

w^{\prime}=\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}))
.
It is clear that

w^{\prime}\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{\omega}
and
w^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})))
.
It remains to show that

w\preceq_{\sim}^{\omega}w^{\prime}
.
By the definition of
\preceq_{\sim}^{\omega}
, it further suffices to show that:

\forall u\leq w,~\exists u^{\prime}\leq w^{\prime},v\in\Sigma^{*}.~uv\sim u^{\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
u
be an arbitrary prefix of
w
.
Because by definition
\rho^{\prime}\in I(u)
, it holds that

u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

For each role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
, let
\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
be defined as the largest prefix of
\rho^{\prime}
such that

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Such a run is well-defined by the fact that
u
is a prefix of an infinite word
w
, and there exists a longer prefix
v
such that
u\leq v
and

v{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
be the role with the maximum
|\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}|
in
\mathcal{P}
.
Let
u^{\prime}=\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}))
.
Clearly,
u^{\prime}\leq w^{\prime}
.
Because
u^{\prime}
is
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}))
for the role with the longest
\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
, it holds for all roles
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
that

u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq u^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Then, there must exist
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{*}
such that

u{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\cdot y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=u^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
be defined in this way for each role.
We construct
v\in\Sigma^{*}
such that
uv\sim u^{\prime}
.
Let
v
be initialized with
\varepsilon
.
If there exists some role in
\mathcal{P}
such that
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}[0]\in\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},!}
, append
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
to
v
and update
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
If not, for all roles
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}[0]\in\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},?}
.
Each symbol
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}[0]
for all roles appears in
u^{\prime}
.
Let
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
denote for each role the index in
u^{\prime}
such that
u^{\prime}[i]=y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}[0]
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
be the role with the minimum index
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
Append
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
to
v
and update
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
Termination is guaranteed by the strictly decreasing measure of
\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}|y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}|
.

We argue that
uv
satisfies the inductive invariant of channel compliancy.
In the case where
v
is extended with a send action, channel compliancy is trivially re-established.
In the receive case, channel compliancy is re-established by the fact that the append order for receive actions follows that in
u^{\prime}
, which is channel-compliant by construction.
We conclude that
uv\sim u^{\prime}
by applying
[
28
, Lemma 22]
.

∎

Lemma 9

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
be a role,
\mathbf{G}
be a global type,

G^{\prime}
be a syntactic subterm of
\mathbf{G}
, and

\{\!\!\{{\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

be its subset construction.
Let
s
be some state in
Q_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
with
G^{\prime}\in s
.
Then, there is a run
\rho_{\mathbf{G}}
in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
ending in state
q_{G}^{\prime}
,
i.e.

\rho_{\mathbf{G}}=q_{0,\mathbf{G}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{\mathbf{G}})}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}q_{G}^{\prime},

such that
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
will reach
s
on the projected trace, i.e.,

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=s_{0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho_{\mathbf{G}})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s.

Proof

Recall that the set of states for
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
is defined as a least fixed point:

Q_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\coloneq\mathrm{lfp}_{\{s_{0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\}}^{\subseteq}\lambda Q.\,Q\cup\{\delta(s,a)\mid s\in Q\land a\in\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\}\setminus\{\emptyset\}

where
\delta(s,a)
is an intermediate transition relation that is defined for all subsets
s\subseteq Q_{\mathbf{G}}
and every event
a\in\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
as follows:

\delta(s,a)\coloneq\{q^{\prime}\in Q_{\mathbf{G}}\mid\exists q\in s,q\xrightarrow{a}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}q^{\prime}\in\delta_{\downarrow}\}

From the definition of
Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, there exists a sequence of states
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
such that
s_{1}=s_{0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
s_{n}=s
and for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
, it holds that

\exists a\in\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}.~\delta(s_{i},a)=s_{i+1}

Let
a_{i}
denote the existential witness for each
i
.
From the definition of
\delta(s,a)
, for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
, it follows that

\forall q^{\prime}\in s_{i+1}.~\exists q\in s_{i}.~q\xrightarrow{a_{i}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}q^{\prime}\in\delta_{\downarrow}

By assumption,
G^{\prime}\in s
.
There then exists a sequence of global syntactic subterms
G_{1},\ldots,G_{n}
such that
G_{1}=\mathbf{G}
,
G_{n}=G^{\prime}
and for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
, it holds that

G_{i}\in s_{i}\land G_{i+1}\in s_{i+1}\land G_{i}\xrightarrow{a_{i}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}G_{i+1}\in\delta_{\downarrow}

We can expand
\varepsilon^{*}
:
for every
i\in\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
, there exists
k_{i}\geq 0
and a sequence of syntactic subterms
G_{i,0},\ldots,G_{i,k_{i}}
such that
G_{i,0}=G_{i}
and
G_{i,k_{i}}=G_{i+1}
and

G_{i,0}\xrightarrow{a}G_{i,1}\in\delta_{\downarrow}\text{ and }G_{i,j}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}G_{i,j+1}\in\delta_{\downarrow}\text{ for every }j\in\{1,k_{i}-1\}.

This expansion yields a run
\rho_{\downarrow}
in the projection by erasure
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})\negmedspace\!\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Because of recursion terms, the expansion might not be unique, but we can pick the smallest
k_{i}
possible for every
i
.
With the definition of
\delta_{\downarrow}
, it is trivial to translate this run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})\negmedspace\!\downarrow_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
to a run
\rho_{\mathbf{G}}
in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
:
the events
a\in\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
become
a^{\prime}\in Σ_{\mathit{sync}}
such that

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(a^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=a

and
\varepsilon
becomes
b\in Σ_{\mathit{sync}}
such that

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(b){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\varepsilon
.

It is clear by construction that
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
(with its corresponding transitions) serves as a witness for
\rho_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, while
G_{1,0},\ldots,G_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,G_{n}
(with its respective transitions) serves as a witness for
\rho_{\mathbf{G}}
.

∎

Lemma 10

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a global type and let
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implement
\mathbf{G}
with
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=(Q_{B,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}},\delta_{B,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}},s_{B,0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}},F_{B,0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
for role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
Let
s\in Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
,
x\in\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
s\xrightarrow{x}t\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
from the subset construction
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
.
Let
u\in\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{*}
such that
s_{0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\xrightarrow{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s
.
Then, there exists
s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\in Q_{B,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
such that
s_{B,0,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\xrightarrow{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s^{\prime}
and
s^{\prime}\xrightarrow{x}t^{\prime}\in\delta_{B,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

Proof

Because
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathbf{G}
, it must hold that
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
since
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
must produce at least the behaviors as specified by
\mathbf{G}
for its role.
It follows that
\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})\subseteq\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
.
From
Lemma

1
, we know that
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
. By construction of
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
, if
ux
is reachable from the initial state in
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
then
ux
is the prefix of some word in
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Therefore, it holds that
ux\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
and consequently
ux\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}))
.
Because
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
is deterministic, there exists a unique
t^{\prime}
such that
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
reaches
t^{\prime}
from the initial state on
ux
.
This concludes our proof.
