7.1

Proof

From the fact that
\mathbf{G}
is implementable, we know there exists a CSM
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
that implements
\mathbf{G}
.
Showing that the subset projection is defined amounts to showing that Send and Receive Validity (
Definitions

5
and
6
) hold for the subset construction.

We proceed by contradiction and assume the negation of Send and Receive Validity in turn, and in each case derive a contradiction to the fact that
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathbf{G}
.
Specifically, we contradict protocol fidelity (
Definition

1
(
i
)), and show that
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})\neq\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
.

To prove the inequality of the two languages, it suffices to prove the inequality of their respective prefix sets, i.e.

\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})\}\neq\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})\}

Specifically, we show there is
v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
such that

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\in\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})\}~\land

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\notin\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})\}\kern 5.0pt.

Because
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is deadlock-free by assumption, every trace either can be extended to end in a final configuration or to be infinite. Therefore, any word
v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
that is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is a member of the prefix set, i.e.

\exists~(\vec{s},\xi).~(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{v}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)\implies v\in\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}})\}\kern 5.0pt.

By the semantics of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
, for any
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
, there exists
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G}))
with
w\sim\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(w^{\prime})
.
For any
w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G}))
, it is straightforward that
I(\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(w^{\prime}))\neq\emptyset
.
Because intersection sets are closed under the indistinguishability relation (Corollary
3
), it holds that
I(w)\neq\emptyset
.
Because
I(\hbox{-})
is monotonically decreasing, if
I(w)
is non-empty then for any
v\leq w
,
I(v)
is non-empty.
By the following, to show that a word
v
is not a member of the prefix set of
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
it suffices to show that
I(v)
is empty:

\forall v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}.~I(v)=\emptyset\implies\forall w.~v\leq w\implies w\notin\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore, under the assumption of the negation of Send or Receive Validity respectively, we explicitly construct a witness
v_{0}
satisfying:

1.

v_{0}
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, and

2.

I(v_{0})=\emptyset
.

Send Validity (
Definition

5
).

Assume that Send Validity does not hold for some role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Let
s\in Q_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
be a state and
s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}s^{\prime}\in\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
a transition in the subset construction
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
such that

\operatorname{tr-orig}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}s^{\prime})\neq s\kern 5.0pt.

Let
D
denote
s\setminus\operatorname{tr-orig}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}s^{\prime})
.
By the negation of Send Validity,
D
is non-empty.
Let
G^{\prime}
be a syntactic subterm in
D
.

Because
G^{\prime}\in s
, it follows from Lemma
9
that there exists
\alpha
such that
\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}
is a run in
\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.
Let
\bar{w}
be
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}))
.
Because
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathbf{G}
, there exists a configuration
(\vec{t},\xi)
of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
such that

(\vec{t}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\bar{w}}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{t},\xi)
.
Instantiating
Lemma

10
with
s
,
s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}s^{\prime}
and
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, it follows that
\vec{t}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
has an outgoing transition labeled
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
. Let
\vec{t}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}t^{\prime\prime}
be this transition.

The send transitions of any local machine in a CSM are always enabled. Formally, for all

w\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
,
x\in\Sigma_{!}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
, if

w
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
and

\vec{t}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\xrightarrow{x}\vec{t}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{t}^{\prime}_{w,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
, then

wx
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
Instantiating this fact with
\bar{w}
and
\vec{t}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}t^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain that
\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

Let
\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
be our witness
v_{0}
; it then follows that
v_{0}
satisfies
1
.
It remains to show that
v_{0}
satisfies
2
, namely
I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m)=\emptyset
.

Claim.
All runs in
I(\bar{w})
begin with
\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}
.

Proof of Claim.

Recall that
\bar{w}
is defined as
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}))
.
Assume by contradiction that
\rho^{\prime}\in I(\bar{w})
and
\rho^{\prime}
does not begin with
\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}
.
Due to the syntactic structure of global runs, the first divergence between two runs must correspond to a syntactic subterm of the form
\sum_{i∈I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{\prime}_{i}\!:\!m^{\prime}_{i}.G^{\prime}_{i}
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}
be the sender in the first divergence between
\rho^{\prime}
and
\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}
, and let the two runs respectively contain the subterms
G^{\prime}_{i}
and
G^{\prime}_{j}
.
Because
\rho^{\prime}
is in
\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}(\bar{w})
, it holds that

\bar{w}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}
.
Because
\bar{w}=\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime}))
, we can rewrite the inequality as

\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}\leq\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}
.

We know that
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha\cdot G^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}
and
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}}}
share a common prefix, followed by different send actions from
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}
, i.e., they are respectively of the form
x^{\prime}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{j}!m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot y^{\prime}
and
x^{\prime}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m^{\prime}_{i}\cdot z^{\prime}
.
We arrive at a contradiction.

End Proof of Claim.

Recall that
G^{\prime}\in D
and
D=s\setminus\operatorname{tr-orig}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m}s^{\prime})
.
By the definition of
\operatorname{tr-orig}(-)
(
Definition

3
), there does not exist a global syntactic subterm
G^{\prime\prime}
with

G^{\prime}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}G^{\prime\prime}\in\delta_{\mathbf{G}}
such that
l^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
.
Therefore, there does not exist a maximal run in
\operatorname{R}^{\mathbf{G}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m)
, and

I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m)=\emptyset

follows.

Our witness
v_{0}=\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}!m
thus satisfies both conditions

1
and
2
required for a contradiction. This concludes our proof that Send Validity is required to hold.

Receive Validity (
Definition

6
).

Assume that Receive Validity does not hold for some role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
.
In other words, there exists
s\in Q_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
with two transitions

s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}}s_{1}
,

s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2}\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}

and
G_{2}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2})

such that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\land{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G_{2}\ldots)}\kern 5.0pt.

Claim I.
There exists
u\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
such that both

u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}

and

u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}

are traces of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof of Claim I.

By the negation of Receive Validity,

G_{2}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2})\subseteq s_{2}
.
From
Lemma

9
for
s_{2}
and
G_{2}\in s_{2}
,
there exists
\rho^{\prime}
such that
\rho^{\prime}
ends in
G_{2}
and is a run in

\mathsf{GAut}(\mathbf{G})
.
Because
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}))
is a prefix in
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{G})
and by assumption
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathbf{G}
, there exists a
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration
(\vec{t},\xi)
such that

(\vec{t}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}))}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{t},\xi)
.
By the subset construction, it holds that
\mathscr{C}(\mathbf{G},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
reaches
s
on
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime}))
.
Instantiating
Lemma

10
twice with
s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}}s_{1}
,

s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2}
and
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, we obtain
t_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}}t_{1}^{\prime}
and
t_{2}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}t_{2}^{\prime}
.
From the determinacy of
B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, it holds that
t_{1}=t_{2}
.
Therefore, it holds that
\vec{t}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=t_{1}
and there exist two outgoing transitions from
\vec{t}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
labeled with
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}
.

From the fact that

s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2}\in\delta_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
,
there exist
G_{1}\in s
and
G_{2}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{tr-dest}(s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}}s_{2})\subseteq s_{2}
such that

G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}^{\prime}\in\delta_{\mathbf{G}}
.
Either
G_{2}=G_{2}^{\prime}
, or
G_{2}
is reachable from
G_{2}^{\prime}
via
\varepsilon
-transitions for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
G_{2}=G_{2}^{\prime}
; if
G_{2}^{\prime}\neq G_{2}
then
G_{2}^{\prime}
can also be picked as the witness from the definition of
M
.
We rewrite
\rho^{\prime}
as follows:

\rho^{\prime}\coloneq\alpha\cdot G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}

From the negation of Receive Validity, we know that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G_{2}\ldots)}

Then, there exists some suffix
\beta
such that the transition

\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{1}}

occurs in
\beta
and

\alpha\cdot G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}\cdot\beta

is a maximal run.
Let
\rho
denote this maximal run.
Let

G_{3}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{1}}G_{4}

be the earliest occurrence of

\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{1}}

in
\beta
.
We rewrite the suffix
\beta
in
\rho
to reflect the existence of
G_{3},G_{4}
:

\rho\coloneq\alpha\cdot G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}\cdot\beta_{1}\cdot G_{3}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{1}}G_{4}\cdot\beta_{2}

Note that
\beta_{1}
does not contain any transitions of the form
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{1}}
.

Let
\bar{w}
denote
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha))
, and
\bar{v}
denote
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\beta_{1}))
.
To produce a witness for
u
, we show that

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}

is a trace of

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
,
and in the resulting CSM configuration
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})
,

\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
has two outgoing transitions labeled

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}
.
Moreover, we show that the channels

\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
and
\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

respectively contain the messages
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
at the head.

First, we show that

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}

is a trace of

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

By assumption that
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathbf{G}
, both

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}

and

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}

are traces of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
Let
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})
and
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime\prime})
respectively denote
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configurations such that

(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}}(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}\cdot\bar{v}}(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

Because send actions are always enabled in a CSM, it suffices to show that
\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}
has an outgoing transition label
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}
.
We do so by showing that

\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}=\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}
:
it is clear from the fact that

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}\cdot\bar{v}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}

is a trace of

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}

that

\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}

has an outgoing transition label

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}
.

Due to the determinacy of subset construction, it suffices to show that

(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}}=(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}\cdot\bar{v}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}}\kern 5.0pt.

This equality follows from the definition of
M
and the fact that

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}\in M^{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{(G_{2}\ldots)}
: because the blocked set of roles in
M
monotonically increases, and for any
G^{\prime},B
, no actions in a run suffix starting with
G^{\prime}
involving roles in
B^{\prime}
are included in
M^{B^{\prime}}_{G^{\prime}}
,
we know that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}
must be the lexicographically earliest action involving
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}
in

\bar{v}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}
.
In other words,

\bar{v}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}}}=\varepsilon
.

This concludes the reasoning that

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}

is a trace of

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

Recall that

(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

is the

\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration reached on

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}
.
We showed above that

\vec{s}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}

has two outgoing transitions labeled

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}
.
It follows from the equality below that
\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
likewise has two outgoing transitions labeled
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}
:

(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}=(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}){\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

We now show that the channels

\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})
and
\xi^{\prime}({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}})

respectively contain the messages
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
at the head.
Recall that
\bar{w}
is defined as
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\alpha))
; this from the fact that
\xi_{\bar{w}}
is uniquely determined by
\bar{w}
and all channels in
\xi_{\bar{w}}
are empty.

Let
u\coloneq\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{1}
.
This concludes our proof that both

u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}

and

u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}

are traces of
\{\!\!\{B_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}\!\!\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.

End Proof of Claim I.

The next claim establishes that our witness
u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}
satisfies
2
.

Claim II.
It holds that
I(u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1})=\emptyset
.

Proof of Claim II.

This claim follows trivially from the observation that every run in

I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2})

must begin with

\alpha\cdot G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}
.
Because

I(u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1})\subseteq I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2})
,
and the
\operatorname{\texttt{{split}}}(\operatorname{\texttt{{trace}}}(\hbox{-}))
of every run in

I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2})

starts with

\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}?m_{2}
,
therefore

I(u\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1})

is empty.

End Proof of Claim II.

From here, the reasoning that every run in

I(\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}!m_{2})
must begin with

\alpha\cdot G_{1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{2}\xrightarrow{}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}:m_{2}}G_{2}

is identical to the reasoning for the analogous claim in the Send Validity case, and thus omitted.

By choosing

v_{0}\coloneq\bar{u}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{1}?m_{1}
,
we thus establish both conditions

1
and
2
required for a contradiction. This concludes our proof that Receive Validity is required to hold.

∎

Figure 4

Two protocol specifications for the same protocol

0.E.2
Entailed Properties from the Literature – Detailed Analysis

For systems with two roles, an
unspecified reception

[
13
, Def. 12]

occurs if a receiver cannot receive the first message in its (only) channel.
Intuitively, this yields a deadlock for a binary system or an execution in which the other role will send messages indefinitely.
For multi-party systems with sender-driven choice, this is not necessarily the case and our receive validity condition ensures that a message in a role’s channel can appear but will not confuse the receiver regarding which choices were made.
Thus, we could lift the property to multiparty systems with a universal quantification over channels.
Our subset projection would prevent this lifted version of unspecified receptions because of deadlock freedom and the fact that any non-deadlock configuration can be extended in a way that the unspecified reception can eventually be received.
Similarly, our subset projection ensures the
absence of orphan messages

[
20
, Sec. 2]
[
8
, Sec. 3]
.
However, we need to adapt the definition to our setting.
For most MST frameworks, the following two types of CSM configurations are equivalent:
final-state configurations, i.e., where each role is in a final state; and
sink-state configurations, i.e., where each role is in a state without outgoing transitions.

Orphan messages
have been defined using final-state configurations.
Our subset projection is more expressive so both configuration types do not necessarily coincide.
Our soundness proof ensures the absence of orphan messages in sink-state configurations and ensures that messages in final-state configurations can be received eventually.
We refer to
Section

11
for a discussion on the expressiveness of local types and FSMs.

The standard notion of
progress

[
17
, Sec. 1]
asks that every sent message is eventually received and every process waiting for a message eventually receives one.
We proved our subset projection sound for finite as well as infinite CSM runs.
For finite runs, both properties trivially hold.
For infinite runs, our subset projection ensures that both is possible but one would require fairness assumptions to ensure that it will actually happen as is common for liveness properties.

In our subset projection, it is also guaranteed that each transition of a local implementation can be fired in some execution of the subset projection.
This is called
executable
by Cécé and Finkel
[
13
, Def. 12]
while it is the property
live
in work by Scalas and Yoshida
[
33
, Fig.5(3)]
and called
liveness
by Barbanera et al.
[
6
, Def. 2.9]
.

A CSM has the
stable property
if any reachable configuration has a transition sequence to a configuration with empty channels.
With our proof technique, we showed every run of a CSM has a common path in the protocol that complies with all participants’ local observations of the run.
There is a furthest point in this path and it is possible that all participants catch up to this point, having empty channels.

Scalas and Yoshida
[
33
]
also consider two properties that are rather protocol-specific than implementation-specific, i.e., protocol fidelity and deadlock freedom trivially ensure that every implementation satisfies these properties if the protocol does.
First, a global type is
terminating

[
33
, Fig.5(2)]
if every CSM run is finite.
It is trivial that this is only true if there are no (used) recursion variable binders
\mu t
.
Second, a global type is
never-terminating

[
33
, Fig.5(3)]
if every CSM run is infinite.
Consequently, this is only the case if the global type has no term
0
.
